# Review log: Session suspend, idle suspension, and resume driver

## Standing context

**Changelog (compaction pass 2, 2026-10-09).** This pass read the whole ledger: the podless-coordination and credential-lease mechanism entries, redesign applies 1, 2, and 5 (redesign 5 is the session-row read-authority redesign of the redesign-6 run), non-spec rounds 17 and 18 of the redesign-3 run, the redesign-4 spec loop (rounds 1 to 8), the redesign-5 spec loop (rounds 1 to 6), spec-recheck and spec-recheck-2, non-spec-recheck rounds 1 to 5 and non-spec-recheck-2, the redesign-5 non-spec loop (rounds 1 to 8), the redesign-6 spec loop (rounds 1 to 4), reconcile.deliverables 1 and 2, the f1 and f2 entries, and redesign-6 non-spec round 1. It rewrote all four sections.
- Lifted: every live DECISION, FACT, WATCHOUT, MISTAKE, OPEN, UNVERIFIED, and unclosed DEFERRED. Facts repeated across rounds (anchor resolution, identifier sweeps, plan map, §29.3 podless coverage, `Gate.Run` relaunch, replica-ID suffix, DLQ key with no EXPIRE, Sweeper per-row `leases.Get`) are merged into one line each.
- Corrected through CORRECTS: **Lease retention.** (adoption needs a pod for `running`/`input_required`/`suspended`); **DLQ TTL home.** (clock is the later of acceptance and recovering-state entry, and covers durable-inbox entries); **Budget keys.** and **`RearmTree` rules.** (no SCAN, re-arm also on leaving `awaiting_client_action`); **`clearInboxOnResume`.** (SPEC-5z item 13 trigger); **Refuted: store-outage degradation.** (admission write and peek stall were real and are fixed); the lock rationale of the funnel and suspension releases (generation scope protects leases); the openapi entry of **Not filed, do not refile.**
- Closed and dropped: the Open entries on Decision 23 (OD-C1) and on §12.8 erasure (OD-C2), both resolved by [redesign5.apply.1]; OD-P1 to OD-P4 and OD-C3; the Deferred TEST-1 claim-map entry (applied by [reconcile.deliverables.1]); every DEFERRED and OPEN that [reconcile.deliverables.2], [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-G1.1], or [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-design-G2.1] marks applied (`InboxDrainFailure` Description and label test, concepts.md CredentialRouter sentence, credential-pool-exhaustion runbook, SPEC-5o clause); the on-demand adoption OPEN; the `errResumeHoldStale` UNVERIFIED (CODE-4 item 8 deletes it); the lease-store-without-binder UNVERIFIEDs (answered: Compose Mode).
- Deleted superseded traps: "Gate tests use a podless row", the on-demand adopter replay-buffer trap, "do not file OD-P4", the `OnReleased` observer traps, the `scanTree` trap, and "`PodExecutor.Release` on an unbound session revokes no new leases" (true only before the publish).
- Disagreement kept newer: none unresolved; each conflict in this window was settled by a later CORRECTS.
- Target: not reached. The section is 277 lines; the previous pass left 252 and this window's ledger added about 1,400 lines of entries to lift. Traps carries every WATCHOUT and MISTAKE that a later round cited or that guards a withdrawn option; cutting them would lose traps that rounds in this window walked into.

### Settled

**Incarnation identity.** A `resuming` incarnation is the `coordination_generation` value its `resume_pending → resuming` write increments to; every non-terminal exit is conditioned on `resuming` at that value (SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**). Rejected: state plus unchanged generation (ABA), `UpdatedAt` token, `recovery_generation`, a new column.
**Deleted SPEC-1b claims.** "At most one attempt per session at a time" and "an attempt ends by the `resuming` watchdog deadline" are deleted.
**`sweepResuming` exit.** Guarded on state plus the listed row's generation; `errResumingSuperseded` on a miss (CODE-4 item 15).
**Entry CAS.** The entry write increments under CAS on the value read before the lease was confirmed.
**Single gateway releaser.** The replacement pod is in neither `PodAssignment` nor the registry before the commit (CODE-4 item 4).
**Orphan-claim GC.** Reclaims a stopped attempt's pod; draining a live attempt older than `claimOrphanTimeout` is accepted in SPEC-1b; `Binder.Resume` claims a fresh idle pod, so the statement holds on slot pools too.
**No post-exit bump.** `bumpCoordinationGenerationOnSnapshotClose` is deleted.
**Generation incrementers.** Takeover CAS (§10.1.2 step 1), resume entry (SPEC-1b), mid-resume terminal collapse (§7.2 step 4(a)); §10.1.1 (SPEC-1f item 2) lists the first two; the terminal bump stays in §7.2.
**§4.2 pointer.** SPEC-1f item 1: field description plus bare pointer "See §10.1.1 **Generation counters:**."
**Never-advanced stamp.** SPEC-1f item 2 keeps the baseline clause, reworded to "a session that no takeover and no resume driver write has advanced".
**§10.1.5 trigger.** Only a pod's generation-stale rejection triggers §10.1.5 (SPEC-1f items 3, 4).
**§10.1.5 no-pod paragraph.** SPEC-1f item 3 adds **Session with no bound pod.**; its residual sentence on stale pod releases is gone (generation scope, see **Pod-release lease scope.**).
**Sweeper generation.** Compares the row with the binding's stamp; `RecordHandoff` is a CAS on the listed row's generation and checks `adoptedState` on the locked row (CODE-5 items 2a, 2b); `renewedGeneration` deleted.
**Adoption read.** SPEC-2b widens §10.1.2 step 0 to read `state` and `pod_assignment`; §29.8 step 4 cites step 0.
**`resuming` not adopted.** Every replica evaluates the §6.2 `resuming` watchdog from the row.
**Lease retention.** SPEC-2a is the one home: renewal while a pod is bound or driver work is pending (`starting`, `running`, `input_required`, held-pod `suspended`, `resume_pending`, `resuming`) and in `awaiting_client_action` (0091 retention, 900 s cap); podless `suspended`, `created`, `finalizing`, `ready` hold none; adoption covers `resume_pending`, `awaiting_client_action`, and any `running`, `input_required`, or `suspended` row that names a pod.
**Podless §29.3 rows.** Every route a podless `suspended` session admits has a non-coordinator server: messages (SPEC-5l), resume (SPEC-3j), terminate and DELETE (SPEC-2d item 2; DELETE inherits "the same states"), resolution (SPEC-2d item 3), events JSON and streaming (relay, SPEC-2d items 4, 5), logs (EventStore); interrupt and upload admit `running` only. No lease code is owed: terminate, delete, and resolution handlers read no lease.
**SPEC-2d item 5.** The §15.1 events-endpoint cell names the `CH-EVENTRELAY` backlog (resolves OD-P4); RECORDS-1 7(o) files the §10.4 reattach buffer only.
**Terminate state cell.** SPEC-2d item 2's cell is the intersection of §15.1 terminate preconditions (no `input_required`, no `resuming`) with SPEC-2a; it is not a copy.
**Sweeper mirror release.** The Sweeper releases a podless row's mirror on each sweep while it is the prior holder (CODE-5 item 1, OD-P1); the skip sits before `eligible` and before the dead-connection branch, after `leases.Get` (it needs `priorHolder`).
**Readopt binding.** `readoptAndFence` resolves the pool pinned to `row.PoolRef` and sets `SlotID = sessionID` when `MaxConcurrentSessions > 1`, plus `Recycle`, `CleanupCommands`, `CleanupTimeoutSeconds` (CODE-5 item 2).
**No on-demand adoption.** None exists; no podless forward exists.
**Fence-skip home.** §10.1.2 step 2 (SPEC-2b) is the one home of the podless skip; steps 0 and 1 still run.
**SPEC-2c sites.** Fence universals become §10.1.2 citations (§28.5.1 CH-ATTACH and CH-FENCE, §28.6, §28.8 rows, §29.4 step 4, §29.5 step 3, §4.7.1 `CoordinatorFence` row, CH-CHECKPOINT/CH-BARRIER Preconditions); item 2 makes CH-FENCE **Exclusivity.** "announced to a bound pod by this channel"; item 8 makes the §28.8 CH-FENCE row "announces the holder change to a bound pod (§10.1.2 step 2)".
**SPEC-1a2.** The §28.5.1 CH-ATTACH **Degradation.** bullet reads "is restored onto a replacement pod by the resume driver when".
**SPEC-1a step 3e.** Rebuild applies to "a session that has neither a checkpoint nor a §4.4.5 minimal state record"; SPEC-1c **Retry policy:** points at the §7.3 resume flow.
**No fence at bind.** The driver's writes are generation-conditioned; code fences after the commit (CODE-4 item 5.4).
**Pre-attach collapse.** SPEC-1e keys it on the `resume_pending → resuming` write.
**Post-claim failure reason.** SPEC-1b item 3 is the one home: `setup_command_failed` for a deterministic setup failure (gRPC `FailedPrecondition`), `runtime_crash` otherwise; the §7.3 classifier (per-session overridable) picks the §6.2 edge. SPEC-3b, SPEC-3c, and the Edge-cases bullet only point at it.
**SPEC-1c relabel.** §6.2's first `resuming` bullet is "Retryable failure during `resuming`"; the non-retryable example is a setup-command failure during a snapshotless rebuild.
**Retry accounting.** SPEC-1b **Retry accounting.** spends a retry on a pod failure, a post-entry driver failure, or the watchdog; nothing else.
**Route list home.** The routes into recovery are listed once, in the **Resume driver.** opening.
**SPEC-3o.** A pointer at **Resume driver.** items 1 and 2.
**§29.6 podless resume.** Untraced entry point (SPEC-3k, SPEC-3m).
**Traversal home.** §8.10 owns delegation-tree traversal.
**SPEC-3b frame.** REST resume row names `status_change`; `state_change` is the adapter-facing kind.
**Held-pod resume.** No pod RPC; the coordinator writes `suspended → running` and delivers per path 6.
**Off-holder held-pod `/resume`.** Fails closed with `TARGET_NOT_READY`, details `currentState` alone (CODE-4 item 8); MCP `resume_session` proxies the same route.
**Owner answers.** OD-2 (Decision 20), OD-3 (21), OD-B immediate `resume_pending` (22), OD-R1/OD-C1 delivery at the pre-running commit (23), OD-1 watchdog bullet unchanged (anchor drift in **Watchdog anchor.**), OD-A synchronous `StampSync` (withdrawn, Decision 25), OD-R2 durable-inbox `EXPIRE` deleted.
**Redesign-6 resolutions.** OD-P3 as the gateway-leader backlog re-check; OD-C2 as §12.8 erasure (SPEC-5aa, Decision 24, CODE-6 item 17); OD-C3 as the generation-scoped pod release (SPEC-1g, SPEC-1g3, CODE-4 item 24). OD-S1 is the only open owner decision.
**`ReattachNode`.** Stops calling `resumeOnPod` (CODE-4 item 11).
**Custody home.** SPEC-5i path 6: synchronous delivery only for a REST message the coordinator serves while the row names a pod; every other message is held in the DLQ before any state write; a podless hold is followed by the guarded `resume_pending` write.
**Backlog resume.** SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** is the one home of the mechanisms: the coordinator's sweep for a held-pod row, the release's one check, and the gateway leader's re-check for podless rows (CODE-6 item 16, interval `gateway.suspendedBacklogRecheckSeconds` minimum 30 s, migration 0183 partial index `idx_sessions_podless_suspended`). The leader evaluates at once on Lease acquisition (`Run` calls `Pass` first), so failover delays it by at most the §12.5 window (25 s). Exclusivity is scoped to "that condition".
**Backlog predicate.** Counts the inbox only for an `Idle` suspension and otherwise only DLQ entries accepted after `SuspendedAt` (`HeldLen`, `CountAcceptedAfter`).
**Release-time inbox migration.** SPEC-5b step 2 order is guarded clear, migrate the in-memory inbox to the DLQ, release; §7.2 migration paragraph (SPEC-5z item 11) is the home of both triggers and the failure outcome; `MigrateInboxToDLQ` gains a state parameter (`session_state` label `suspended` at the release).
**Drain-failure rows.** SPEC-5q drops the `resume_pending` qualifier from §16.1 and §16.5; CODE-6 item 4 step 3 rewrites the `InboxDrainFailure` Description in rules.go and runs `make generate` (docs/alerting/rules.yaml, charts/lenny/files/alerting-rules.yaml, manifests.yaml).
**Adapter seal.** `SealSession` is the one turn check. The idle suspension seals, checkpoints, and writes `running → suspended` with the pod cleared, then releases. Within one coordinator incarnation only the suspension's own non-landing outcomes lift the seal. Another suspension attempt lifts it only after a takeover has advanced `coordination_generation`, which the suspend write's condition excludes. Entry removal ends the seal, and a `CoordinatorFence` does not. Redesign-7 OD-1 is withdrawn: no mid-turn pod release, including during a Postgres failover. Redesign-7 OD-2 is settled: `interrupt_acknowledged` changes no count.
**Cross-replica slot release.** Refuted. A release clears the pod only after its checkpoint wrote `WorkspaceSnapshot` (pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:438-458). Every later restore therefore takes `Binder.Resume` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go:37-46, :102), which claims an `Idle`-phase pod or rebinds a scrubbed reserved hold (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:157-166, :179), never the released session's pod. The releasing replica's unconditional `Shutdown` (pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go) reaches only the old pod's adapter.
**Held-message delivery.** SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.**: peek the older head, remove after `Send`, run at every commit to `running` and before every path-2 delivery, expire nothing; no at-least-once sentence.
**Held read failure.** An inbox or DLQ read failure is not a failed delivery: `deliverBuffered` logs WARN and returns nil (CODE-4 item 6), and the message in hand proceeds under its own path; the sentence sits after the failed-delivery consequences.
**Failed held delivery.** The replica stops and the entry stays at the head.
**Terminal race.** `holdMessage` re-reads with `ReadPrimary` after the enqueue and removes its own entries only on a terminal or failed re-read.
**Receipt home.** **Held messages.** plus **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.**
**§15.4 `delivery` table.** SPEC-5k's sentence below the table overrides the `"queued"` row for `suspended`; do not delete it.
**`inbox_unavailable`.** SPEC-5z item 9 widens it to DLQ enqueue failures and a failed re-read.
**DLQ TTL home.** Last sentence of **Delivery of held messages.**: the TTL of a DLQ entry and of a durable-inbox entry runs from the later of acceptance and entry to the current recovering state, enforced only in recovering states; code gates `sweepDLQExpiry` on `now - row.UpdatedAt >= DefaultDLQTTL` (CODE-6 item 12); SPEC-5z item 4 points the Per-message TTL row there and deletes its activation sentence.
**DLQ scores.** Score is `EnqueuedAt + DefaultDLQTTL` (acceptance order, SPEC-5z items 3 and 12), and the score is no longer the expiry.
**Reason definitions.** §15.4 is the only home.
**Terminal drain.** SPEC-5z item 1 covers every DLQ entry; `DrainOnTerminal` already drains inbox and DLQ.
**`inbox_cleared`.** SPEC-5z item 13 fires it only on adoption where step 0 finds `running`, `input_required`, or held-pod `suspended`; RECORDS-1 7(h) files the emission.
**§29.4 step 9.** SPEC-5y wording; step 9 still traces an interrupt suspension.
**Resume before release.** SPEC-5b carries "unless it resumes the session for held messages"; CODE-6 item 2 runs the backlog resume before the release in the same tick.
**Pod-named condition.** Stated once, in the path 6 lead; code: `resumeHeldPod` guard and `errResumeHeldPodReleased`.
**`send_message` receipt.** `queued` on both branches for a `suspended` target; only a coordinator-served REST message to a held pod gets `delivered`.
**`clearInboxOnResume`.** Deleted.
**No `suspended` forward.** None exists.
**SPEC-5i2 pointers.** Items 1, 3, 4 as staged.
**One payload encoding.** MCP marshals `sessionserver.MessagePayload`; `deliverBuffered` decodes only that.
**Held-delivery context.** Server-lifetime context per entry (CODE-4 item 6).
**PreMessageDelivery.** MCP block moves ahead of `Classify`; `deliverBuffered` runs no interceptor.
**Held REJECT.** Audit row, no publish, remove, continue; audit failure stops the loop.
**PostAgentOutput signature.** As CODE-4 states; `route.go` is a CODE-4 target.
**Refused delivery.** SPEC-5f: a refused held message stays held; others are re-routed by current state.
**Idle atomicity.** The seal and the generation together are the atomicity witness. The suspend write takes effect only while the row is `running`, names the pod, has the listed `LastAgentActivityAt`, and carries `bind.CoordinationGeneration`, and it clears the pod. `classifyIdleWrite` decides every try of that write, first or repeated after a store error, on the locked row alone (CODE-6 item 4 step 3). Rejected: a list of post-error row forms, a `SuspendedAt` identity, and a new column.
**Admission fail-open.** Withdrawn by redesign 9. No admission write exists, so a delivery during a §12.3 failover needs no fail-open, and a stale-row delivery to a sealed session is refused at the adapter.
**Re-route read.** After `executor.ErrSessionSealed` the re-route of the undelivered messages reads the primary (CODE-6 item 7); the first message-path read classifies through `ReadPrimary` under the delivery lock.
**Idle rule home.** SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**
**Idle clock reset.** Stated once in the §6.2 intro; excludes `input_required`.
**`"idle"` trigger.** SPEC-5x deletes it; carriers in CODE-6 item 13.
**Docs mirrors.** DOCS-1b mirrors SPEC-5w; SPEC-5y has none.
**SPEC-5d2.** Deletes the `maxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds` glosses.
**Release guard.** `State == suspended && SuspendedAt.Equal(...) && PodAssignment == ...` (CODE-6 item 4.3).
**Suspension release step 4.** Releases through `releaseReportedBinding(ctx, row.ID, bind, DispositionCompleted)` on the `*BindResult` the binding check read, under `slotAccountLocks`; pointer identity discriminates; the helper gains a disposition parameter and a bool result; a skip emits nothing.
**Budget keys.** SPEC-5o re-arms every key of the root's tree on entry to and exit from `suspended` and when the row leaves `awaiting_client_action` for `resume_pending`; TTL 691200 s.
**Budget-key carrier.** CODE-6 item 14: `RearmTree(ctx, root, parentIDs)` pipelines EXPIRE on named keys with no SCAN; the server lists `ListByRoot` by effective root (`RootSessionID` else `ID`) and passes every listed row's `ParentSessionID`; flag `--delegation-budget-key-ttl-seconds`, chart `delegation.budgetKeyTTLSeconds`, startup refusal when TTL does not exceed the suspended lifetime; `PurgeRoot` keeps its Cluster routing fix (`TestPurgeRootRoutesToHashTagOwner`).
**Interrupt single writer.** MCP tool proxies `/interrupt` (CODE-6 item 1); `handleInterrupt` returns `session.Validate(EndpointInterrupt, r.State)` inside its mutator, answered by `writePreconditionError`.
**Wiring helpers.** `budgetKeyRearmer`, pinned by `TestBudgetRearmerWiring`. `wireTurnAdmitter` is deleted with the admission write.
**Suspended expiry miss.** `sweepSuspended` returns `errSuspendedSuperseded`.
**Session-row read authority.** Non-spec Design **Session-row read authority.** is the one home (rules R1 to R3); `sessionstore.ReadPrimary` is the one primary read; declining mutators return sentinels (`errMaxAgeSuperseded`, `errResumePendingSuperseded`, `errOrphanSuperseded`,  `errIdleWriteNotApplied`); `errHoldReread` is deleted; RECORDS-1 7(k) files the remaining nil-returning mutators.
**Message-path read failures.** Non-spec Design **Message-path read failures.** is the one home for the session-row reads on the message paths (invariants I1 and I2, one table row per read), and it cites CODE-4 items 6 and 7 and CODE-6 items 2, 4, and 16.3 for the other reads. Only the classification read falls back to the entry row. The re-read after a seal refusal and the `bufferAfterResumedSendFailure` re-read read the primary only and take the held branch on any error, and the inbox-on-error branch is deleted. `DeliverHeld` and `ResumeSuspended` take their caller's row and read none, and the delegate_task handler reads the child from the primary after the task input before it calls `DeliverHeld`, which supersedes the ledger DECISIONs that gave `DeliverHeld` a read-pool fallback. A path-2 refusal re-routes once. Do not add a helper that returns a row with its read source.
**Orphan reconciler guard.** `failOrphan`'s mutator writes only when `eligible(*s) && s.PodAssignment == row.PodAssignment` (CODE-6 item 4); it cites §10.1.4.
**Funnel suspended guard.** From a `suspended` source each funnel mutator returns `errReportConflict` unless `r.PodAssignment == row.PodAssignment && s.holdsPod(*r)` (CODE-4 item 22); `TestFailureReportOnReleasedSuspendedRowIsNoOp` is table-driven over the three mutators with admit control rows.
**Design lease gate.** Admits every session when no lease store or no pod binder is wired (Compose Mode); off-holder tests name "a pod binder wired", and release/backlog cases also a registry binding.
**`HeldLen` error.** `ResumeBacklog` logs and returns true; the release's check logs and continues.
**`ReleaseSuspendedPod`.** Off `Tick`, per-replica in-flight set, 60 s checkpoint deadline, context-aware `lockFlight`.
**Idle default fallback.** CODE-6 item 9 deletes the age-cap fallback.
**Release after the claim.** CODE-3 splits `connect` into claim and handshake halves with the hook between; a hook error or post-hook handshake failure releases the claim (`DeleteClaim` on `Claim`/`Bind`, `releaseResumeSlot(leaked=false)` on `Resume`); the nil-hook `Claim` release is unconditional; `Bind` releases only `MintedLeaseIDs`.
**Lost-commit release.** `Binder.ReleaseUncommitted` releases the attempt's own leases.
**Resume credentials.** Option A: the suspension release returns leases; every restore assigns new leases after the hook and handshake and before `cl.Resume` (SPEC-1g, CODE-4 item 20).
**Lease generation.** SPEC-1g: "Each credential lease records a generation." A restore's leases record the generation its entry write set; other leases record 0; a renewal or rotation records the replaced lease's value.
**Pod-release lease scope.** A pod release returns only leases at or below its binding stamp (CODE-4 item 24, `ReleaseThrough`); only `failPhase`, `ReclaimClaimed`, and `revokeFinalizeLease` keep the session-wide release.
**Renewal stop.** CODE-4 item 23: `Renew` refuses with `ErrLeaseReleased` when `LeaseStore.Lookup` finds no record or no pool binding, re-reads after `Assign`, both `ReleaseSession` implementations list twice; `onExtend` advances rows through update-only `Advance`; SPEC-1g3 edits §4.9 renewal step 1.
**Funnel lock.** CODE-4 item 22 keeps `slotAccountLocks` so a same-replica driver's replacement binding is not torn down by the funnel's `releaseExecutor`.
**Single lease acquire.** CODE-4 item 3.3; `publishBinding` on the rebuild branch.
**Release before rebind.** `releaseThenResumeOnPod(ctx, row, onClaimed)`.
**`completeResume` context.** Server-lifetime context.
**Commit repeat.** Item 5.2 repeats on a non-guard store error.
**Commit-miss classification.** Home: item 5.3.
**`bumpRecoveryGeneration`.** Deleted by CODE-4 item 4.
**`holdsPod`.** Defined in CODE-4 item 8 (`PodAssignment != ""` or no binder).
**Binderless driver.** `DriveResume` runs `completeResume` with no binder.
**Over-cap skip.** Lives only in CODE-4 item 1.
**Test landing rule.** A test listed under a code deliverable lands in that step; it may assert only behavior that step or an earlier one builds, and it goes under the step that creates the newest symbol it calls.
**TEST-0.** Test-lane step at S6.
**Migration 0182 test.** Tier 2 `prodMigrationSchema` entry plus CHECK assertion.
**Migration 0183 test.** `{migration: "0183", table: "sessions"}` entry plus `mustHaveIndex`/`mustNotHaveIndex`.
**S12 tiers.** S12 declares tier 2 for the `credleasestore` `Lookup`/`Advance` test.
**`TestStaleReplicaReleaseKeepsPeerRestoreLeases_spec_10_1_5`.** Under CODE-6.
**`TestStartCommitDeliversPreRunningHeld`.** Under CODE-4, asserting redelivery through a later `deliverBuffered`.
**spec-map rule.** Remove deleted tests' entries and add the replacing driver tests.
**Test homes.** As the Testing section states; CODE-1 accrual test visits `starting` and `input_required`.
**Expiry hook test.** Rewritten onto a suspended row.
**Checklist.** S1 to S5 point at SPEC-1 to SPEC-5.
**Chart.** CODE-6 item 11 edits values.yaml only; `gateway` and `delegation` are open Objects.
**openapi descriptions.** Age wording in CODE-1 item 4, idle wording in CODE-6 item 9; `generated_schemas.go` comes from `go generate` in mcptools, outside `make generate`; the `/resume` summary stays unstaged.
**Age docs and help.** As CODE-1 and DOCS-1b name.
**Client-guide targets.** DOCS-1b names them item by item, including the `input_required` timer sentence and the `/resume` preconditions row (DOCS-1a).
**DOCS-1b credential pages.** concepts.md **Credential routing** and **Pool exhaustion**, the credential-pool-exhaustion runbook (frontmatter and Trigger list), and mcp-sdk.md walkthroughs.
**`session_expired` rows.** DOCS-1b rewrites only the `maxClientIdleSeconds` half; `maxSessionAge` stays.
**runtime-configuration.md.** DOCS-1b deletes `limits.maxIdleTimeSeconds`.
**Proposal shape.** One proposal.
**Impact rows.** 0077 Draft; 0079 nothing; 0081 Implemented, SPEC-3g edits its envelope; 0085 Draft, rebase; 0086 Draft, mcp-sdk.md and `postByID` overlap; 0091 retired hold; 0090 row as the baseline wrote it.
**0091 lease behavior.** 0091 changed no REG-COORDLEASE write.
**Budget keys in the tree.** `DefaultTTL` 24 h set only when `TTL == -1`; missing key reads as 0; no settle-time deletion; tree keys are tree_size, tree_memory, tokens, parallel_children:{p}, children_total:{p}, parallel_children_hwm, token_grant.
**`resume_pending` keeps the pod name.** "No bound pod" is read from state.
**Send and context.** `Send` returns the ctx error mid-turn after consumption.
**Gateway-caused stream ends.** `closedByGateway` skips the failure handler; no deadlock on `slotAccountLocks`.
**Checkpoint deadline.** The production checkpointer sets none.
**Commit witness.** `pgstore.Update` can error after a landed COMMIT.
**Replica reads.** `pgstore.Get`, `GetByID`, `List`, `ListByRoot` read `s.read`; `Update` locks on the primary; a mutator error writes nothing; a nil mutator return writes and advances `UpdatedAt`.
**Suspension metrics.** CODE-6 item 4 is the first emitter.
**Anchors and sweeps.** Every staged anchor resolves once with whitespace collapsed; identifier sweeps find every site staged. Do not re-audit without a new diff.
**Plan scope.** The single §10.2 item (plan:2474-2525); F-7.3.31 is not assigned; RECORDS-1 items 3 to 6 dispose of 0091's filings; the expired-pod recycle is declined (item 9).
**Kubernetes surface.** No CRD field, status write, finalizer, webhook, or request-path reconcile; claim DELETEs reuse `podclaim.DeleteClaim`; the leader job uses the existing `lenny-gateway-leader` Lease.
**Lease timing.** Sweeper interval 15 s, lease TTL 60 s; replica IDs carry a random suffix, so a restart never matches `priorHolder`; the Postgres lease fallback lapses on TTL.
**DLQ key.** No key-level EXPIRE exists.
**Leader capacity.** The podless population is bounded by the concurrent-session caps (non-terminal rows count), so a pass is about 10,000 rows per 300 s at Tier 3.
**Adopter turn state.** Closed by the adapter's count (CODE-6 item 18), which survives a handoff and counts a `response` no stream received. Do not reintroduce a row turn boundary, `CloseTurn`, `TurnInFlight`, a gateway-side turn tracker, or an adopter-side close.
**Refuted: second slot for one session ID.** The adapter registry is `map[string]*slotState` keyed by session ID (pkg/adapter/server.go:388), and a claim on a started entry is refused unless it is the idempotent repeat (pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:109-114).
**Refuted: re-emitted `toolCallId` reuses an approval.** `AwaitApproval` re-records the interaction as `PhasePending` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/toolapproval.go:106-116). The pgstore overwrites on the same key (interactionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:73-75, `phase = EXCLUDED.phase`), the memory store replaces the entry (interactionstore.go:141-152), and the waiter registry keeps no verdict after `Resolve` (toolapproval/toolapproval.go:83-95).
**Podless terminate racing a restore.** Confirmed in `handleTransition`: its mutator wrote `completed` over a read-pool `Get` without re-validation (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_transitions.go:63-83), which could complete a `resuming` row that `EndpointTerminate` refuses (pkg/api/v1/session/session.go:297-305). The fix is staged in CODE-6 item 1.
**`AdapterTerminating`.** No gateway code consumes it. The adapter emits it (pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:356), and the gateway names it only in a comment (pkg/gateway/session/orphansession/orphansession.go:9). Before the commit only the attempt holds the replacement pod's connection, so the §10.1.4 transition is the attempt's own failure report and takes the `ExpectedCoordinationGeneration` guard when a consumer is built. Not filed.
**Rule-2 attribution.** Superseded: Design D4 states the listing rule as one invariant and lists no reconcilers ([restructure.non-spec-to-constraints]).
**Refuted: Postgres lease fallback holds a transaction for the lease life.** The fallback takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock` per operation and stores the holder and `expires_at` in `session_leases` (pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go:9-17, 63-65). The `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` wording in §10.1.1 **Per-session coordination:** predates 0092, and this proposal does not file it.
**§27.6 `idle timeout`.** It means the playground login-record idle sweep (pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/auth.go:321-343). That sweep is wired at cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:494-501, which states that the agent-session idle reaper does not reclaim these records. Idle suspension leaves it unchanged.

### Traps

**Do not restore the old SPEC-1b condition, `UpdatedAt` as guard or token, the post-exit bump, or the deleted SPEC-1b claims.** State plus generation is ABA-prone; `AdvanceLastSeq` moves `UpdatedAt`.
**Terminal writers read `fromState` outside their mutator.** `handleTransition` and `handleDelete` read it on the locked row (CODE-6 item 1); other terminal writers remain a §7.2 step 4 defect owned elsewhere.
**Binding stamp.** `resume_rebind.go` stamped `G−1`; CODE-4 item 5.4 restamps with `G`.
**Do not add a `resuming` guard to §10.1.2 step 1.** SPEC-2a's step-0 tie and SPEC-1b exits carry exclusivity; the step-1 0-row path covers the race order.
**Fence-restatement sweeps.** Grep with lines joined; "must have received" hides from "must receive". No §28 site may describe how a podless session acquires the holder role; §28.6 **One holder per session.** is the home, and its "`CH-FENCE` establishes the constraint" stays.
**"No bound pod" is read from state.** Recovering rows name the failed pod; fixtures must carry it.
**Do not describe a pointer's target in SPEC-1f.**
**Replaced: inbox buffering, off-holder forward, `DrainForResume`, `clearInboxOnResume`, drain after commit.** Each lost to duplicate or stranded delivery; do not restore retract-after-drain, expiry in the delivery loop, the DLQ sweep of `suspended` rows, the durable inbox `EXPIRE`, or a backlog resume keyed on an interrupt suspension's inbox.
**Release migration is not the forbidden entry migration.** The trap forbids migrating on entry to `suspended`; the release migrates after the clear. Do not move it to the `running → suspended` write.
**Replaced: migrate before the clear.** A held-pod resume's `deliverBuffered` mid-`Send` would deliver twice. Clear first.
**Accepted migration windows.** A crash loses the in-memory inbox, and a failed post-migration re-read on a terminal session leaves migrated entries with no reclaimer (SPEC-5z item 11). Refile only with a widening path.
**No at-least-once sentence in SPEC-5i.** Do not cite `delivered_message_ids`.
**Never route a refused held entry by path 6 or re-enqueue it.**
**Read failure versus removal failure.** A `LREM`/`ZREM` error stays a returned error; only `Peek`/`PeekOldest` errors return nil. CODE-6 item 7 gets no read-error branch. Under a read failure a new message can overtake older held entries; that ordering loss is accepted.
**Delivery lock.** Do not take it in `SuspendIdle` or `ReleaseSuspendedPod`, do not hold it together with `slotAccountLocks`, and do not call `ResumeSuspended` holding it.
**Replaced: idle atomicity from `TurnInFlight` alone, `TryLock` in `SuspendIdle`.**
**Replaced: the session-row turn boundary (`turn_open`, `turn_seq`, migration 0184, `CloseTurn`, `SetTurnCloser`, `sending`, `admittedSeq`, `TurnInFlight`).** It lost because the gateway cannot see a `response` that no `Attach` stream receives, so a turn a dead coordinator started either pinned the session or was suspended mid-turn.
The adapter's count (CODE-6 item 18) is the only turn state. Do not add a row column, a gateway-side tracker, a close site, or an adopter-side close.
Count at the hub tap on the runtime output's single reader, never in the `Attach` send loop; `linefanout.Hub` drops lines read with no subscriber.
`interrupt_acknowledged` changes no count (redesign-7 OD-2, settled). Any `SealSession` error or refusal leaves the session `running`. Keep the seal check and the count increment in one hold of `s.mu`.
**Replaced: the read-only `AttachTurnState` query, the `StampSync` admission and its fail-open window, and the delivery lock held across the release (redesign 9).** A stale-row delivery to a sealed session is refused by the adapter and held. Do not re-add a gateway admission write, a per-replica marker, or a second turn query at the release.
**Replaced: `SuspendIdle` step 3's branches keyed on an earlier store error, its "counts as landed" predicate, the sentinel `errIdleSuspendSuperseded`, and the unseal on any guard miss (redesign 1).**
They lost because the branch taken depended on the attempt's history rather than on the locked row, the post-error row-form list had no single rule, and the clean-miss unseal fired on a generation miss, where the session already has a newer coordinator or resume incarnation.
`classifyIdleWrite(seen, row, gen)` on the locked row decides every try; Stale (generation other than `bind.CoordinationGeneration`) sends the pod no RPC, evicts the own binding, and logs `coordinator_preempted`. The pod release is not generation-checked, so this conjunct is the only guard against a stale coordinator releasing an adopter's pod.
Do not restore a post-error branch, a `SuspendedAt` identity, a new column, or a `CoordinatorFence` rule that ends the seal; extend the classifier and `TestClassifyIdleWrite_spec_6_2` instead. The unlifted-seal bound has one spec home, SPEC-5ab **Session seal.**; SPEC-5f cites it.
**Replaced: 0091's `renewedGeneration` and unconditional `RecordHandoff`.**
**Replaced: a restore that assigns no credential lease.** Keep `AssignCredentials` after `handshake` and before `cl.Resume`.
**Replaced: `ErrOnClaimed`.** The retry loops stop on an unwrapped hook error.
**Do not add a release inside the shared `connect`.** The Resume slot path releases through `releaseResumeSlot`, never `DeleteClaim`.
**Replaced: the session-wide release on `Bind` failures.**
**Replaced: the lock as lease protection.** The generation scope (CODE-4 item 24) protects a resume's leases; the lock only keeps the replacement binding from `releaseExecutor`. Do not delete the lock or narrow it to slot bindings.
**Replaced: the OD-C3 residual.** Do not return a session-wide `ReleaseSession` from `Release` or `ReleaseSlot`.
**Lease generation value.** Do not write "the session's `coordination_generation` at its assignment"; finalize leases record 0 while the row is at baseline 1.
**Replaced: sandbox-name identity in step 4.** Same-pod slots and re-claimed recycled pods reuse the name; use pointer identity.
**Replaced: the `OnReleased` observer.** It missed a release between mint and track, a `ReleaseSession` snapshot before the replacement, and cross-replica releases. Do not reintroduce an in-process observer.
**Renewal-stop rules.** A nil `w.leases` counts as no record; tests with a nil store must pass a store holding the lease. Do not simplify `Advance` back to `Put` (upsert resurrects released rows). `Lookup` is a new scan path; `scanLease` folds errors into not-found. pgstore `Advance` must move the `expires_at` column. A missing record also follows §11.4 user revocation.
**Replaced: per-site `errHoldReread`.** Use `ReadPrimary`; no `store.Get` read decides a write.
**Replaced: nil-returning declining mutators.** Return a sentinel.
**Guards against a stale listed row.** Compare with the listed row's `PodAssignment` inside the mutator; any write that clears `PodAssignment` and then releases needs a guard in every list-then-write reconciler. Memstore tests cannot see lag; use a stale-`Get` wrapper. The message-path stale-read test must use snapshot podless `suspended` over stored `running`.
**Interrupt guard.** Do not guard on `running` alone; `EndpointInterrupt` admits `input_required`.
**Replaced: per-replica periodic backlog evaluation and the OD-P3 residual.** Do not move the evaluation back onto every watchdog tick; any sentence granting the leader exclusivity must name the condition (the watchdog lifetime sweep runs on every replica). Do not add a SPEC-2a sentence naming the leader evaluation.
**Do not re-add the list of podless resume mechanisms.** CODE-6 items 4 step 3a, 7, 16 are code homes; keep the edge-case label **Held message whose `resume_pending` write does not follow.**
**Replaced: on-demand adoption and lease renewal of podless rows.** Do not re-add an adoption or forward for a podless row; `Upsert` clears `released_at`, so a one-shot mirror release is undone.
**Replaced: migration 0183 with `ListFilter` fields.** 0183 is now the partial index only.
**Index-only migration test.** It must be named under tests/tier2_component/migrations/ with no `columns`; lint-migrations pass 3 fails otherwise.
**`adoptedState` pod conjunct.** A binderless `/start` commits `running` with no pod and no lease. If SPEC-2a's set changes again, cut CODE-5 to a pointer rather than editing both.
**MISTAKE: "reachable only after a bind".** Redesign 5 justified the exclusion that way; binderless `running` rows exist, and the claim would have failed 0060's test.
**Binderless versus no lease store.** "Dev mode" meant no lease store while `holdsPod` means no binder; they diverge in Compose Mode. Check any gate against binderless rows. Fixtures without a binder make `holdsPod` true (`newReportFixture`), so no-op guard tests pass vacuously.
**MISTAKE: renaming a path-6 action in one place.** Grep the whole proposal.
**MISTAKE: recording a regression the proposal causes as a pre-existing defect.** Fix with a code carrier (budget-key TTL, client-cycled root, renewal re-mint, readopt binding).
**MISTAKE: crediting 0091 with a lease keep-and-renew change.**
**MISTAKE: lettered RECORDS-1 cross-references lag.** 7(f) inbox fallback, 7(g) barrier generation, 7(h) `inbox_cleared`, 7(i) crash duplicate, 7(j) durable trimmer, 7(k) nil-returning mutators, 7(l) unindexed listings, 7(m) rebuild bound, 7(n) credential lookup fail-open, 7(o) §10.4 reattach buffer, 7(p) tenant-deletion queue erasure.
**MISTAKE: the `Refuted: store-outage degradation` entry over-reached.** It refuted the admission write's Postgres dependency, which was a real conflict with §12.3; the Redis peek stall was a confirmed finding. The refutation stands only for DLQ message loss under Redis loss.
**MISTAKE: a "fixed" history entry with two halves.** The drain-failure label landed while the Description did not; check every half before trusting a fixed list.
**MISTAKE: `scanTree` and the client-cycled-root acceptance.** Both were withdrawn; the cycle is closed by re-arming on `enterResumePending`.
**MISTAKE: line citations into the proposal's own files.** Deleting a row shifted `spec-changes.md` lines and falsified summary citations. Cite by heading or bold label.
**Glob trap.** `*spec-changes.md` matches `*non-spec-changes.md`; use the full name. A Bash command naming `spec/` paths that writes files is blocked by the spec-lease hook.
**Budget-key "fails closed" is a spec property only.**
**`RearmTree` rules.** Post-commit, best-effort, never inside a mutator; no SCAN; `ParentSessionID` is overloaded by derive, and listing by effective root covers it.

**Lease acquire.** No acquire inside `releaseEarlierBinding` or `releaseThenResumeOnPod`.
**S8 signature.** `onClaimed` added at S8; callers pass nil until S12.
**`recordRouteRejection`.** Nil writer panics.
**Do not add PreMessageDelivery to the REST path.**
**Deadline safety.** Keep step 2's guard and item 3.4's sentence.
**Never treat a non-guard `Update` error in `completeResume` as a guard miss.**
**Landed-test traps.** Keep both conditions; do not import `podHeld`; no "recovery state" exclusion list.

### [non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the tier-8 gap is closed by ONE test, for the Redis held-read outage only, appended to the TEST-1 **Tier 8** bullet: `TestHeldReadOutageDoesNotStallDelivery_spec_7_2`, on the in-process fixture of tests/tier8_chaos/stream_failure_chaos_test.go (`newStreamChaosGateway`) with `Options.Messaging` over a real Redis container reached through that file's `blackholeProxy`, client built by `redisconn.NewUniversalClient`. BECAUSE the partition (reads hang) is the injection that discriminates the stall regression; a container stop gives connection-refused, which the tier-1 fake error already covers — ALTERNATIVES: Kind `kubectl scale lenny-redis --replicas=0` (rejected: the e2e Redis is emptyDir, so the held entry is lost and the "delivered after Redis returns" half cannot hold; also connection-refused, not a hang); `containers.Redis.Stop` (rejected: Terminate, no restart, data lost).
DECISION: no tier-8 Postgres-outage delivery test. BECAUSE the message path reads the row with `sessionstore.ReadPrimary` (an `Update` on the primary) under the delivery lock before `executor.Send` reaches the `StampSync` admitter, so a Postgres outage fails the message before the fail-open is reached; the fail-open is reachable only when the primary read succeeds and the write then fails, which a store outage cannot inject deterministically. Also the e2e Postgres is emptyDir (store_failure_test.go `ensurePostgresSchema` after restore). Tier 1 `TestStampSyncStoreErrorAdmits` stays the pin. — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "with Postgres unavailable, a message to a running session is delivered" (rejected: it cannot pass against the proposal's own message path, nor against today's `loadMessageTarget`, which 500s on a read error, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages.go `loadMessageTarget`).
OPEN: separate finding for a later round, not filed here: Design **Session-row read authority.** rule 1 puts `ReadPrimary` (a primary row lock) on every message path, so during a Postgres primary failover every message to a running session fails, while staged SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** justifies the `StampSync` fail-open by "existing sessions continue during a Postgres failover (§12.3)". Either the fail-open sentence is unreachable in practice or the message-path read needs the same §12.3 treatment. A human or the spec loop should decide.
FACT: tier-8 in-process gateway fixture with a TCP partition proxy exists: tests/tier8_chaos/stream_failure_chaos_test.go `newStreamChaosGateway`, `blackholeProxy`; real-Redis container helper tests/testinfra/containers/redis.go `StartRedis`/`Stop` (Stop terminates, no restart). EVIDENCE: as cited.

### [non-spec.5.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: the message-path classification falls back to the entry row (REST `loadMessageTarget`, MCP `lenny/send_message` `deps.Store.Get`) when `ReadPrimary` returns a non-`ErrNotFound` error; `DeliverHeld` returns nil on its own `ReadPrimary` error — BECAUSE shipped MCP send reads the read pool and makes no primary call before `Executor.Send`, so it delivers through a §12.3 failover, and no state write lands during that window, so the read-pool row is as current as any decision can use. A stale `running` row reaches the `StampSync` fail-open (CODE-6 item 5.5); a stale held row goes through `holdMessage`, whose own failed re-read answers `inbox_unavailable` — ALTERNATIVES: fallback inside `ReadPrimary` (every rule-1 site would decide on a lagging row silently); dropping the primary read (reopens the lagging-`suspended` hold defect redesign 6 fixed); narrowing SPEC-5f to write-only failure (regresses shipped behavior and edits spec to match it); a fresh `store.Get` (the entry row is already in hand); a clause in Design rule 1 (its "(CODE-6 item 7)" already points at the home).
WATCHOUT: the fallback must not extend to the re-read after `sessionidle.ErrSessionSuspended`. The primary has just answered `suspended`; a stale `running` fallback plus a failing `StampSync` delivers to a suspended session. The item 7 sentence forbids it, and no test discriminates it. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 7 lead

### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: the message-path classification read falls back to the entry row the request already read (`loadMessageTarget` on REST, the `deps.Store.Get` target lookup on MCP) when `ReadPrimary` returns an error other than `ErrNotFound`; `DeliverHeld` treats its own `ReadPrimary` error as a held read failure (WARN, nil); the re-read after `ErrSessionSuspended` keeps failing. One sentence in CODE-6 item 7's lead, one clause in the `SessionDelivery` bullet, one case added to `TestMessagePathsClassifyPrimaryRow_spec_7_2`. Rule 1 is untouched: its "(CODE-6 item 7)" already points at the home. BECAUSE during the §12.3 failover window no state write can land, so the read-pool row is as current as any decision can use, and the item 5.5 admitter fail-open becomes the reachable failover contract SPEC-5f states. Reusing the entry row costs no extra `Get`. ALTERNATIVES: (a) make `ReadPrimary` itself fall back to `Get` (rejected: every rule-1 site, including `holdMessage`'s terminal re-read and CAS expected values, would silently decide on a lagging row); (b) drop the primary classification read and rely on the admitter's `ErrSessionSuspended` re-route (rejected: a lagging `suspended` row on a primary-`running` session would hold a message no delivery point picks up, the defect redesign 6 fixed); (c) narrow SPEC-5f's failover rationale (rejected: it regresses shipped MCP delivery through a failover, which §12.3 **Behavior during Postgres failover:** requires); (d) a new fresh `store.Get` on the error (rejected: the entry row is already in hand).
WATCHOUT: do not extend the fallback to the re-read after `sessionidle.ErrSessionSuspended`. That error means the primary answered `suspended`; a read-pool fallback there can classify a stale `running` row and re-send, and with the primary then failing `StampSync` fails open and delivers to a suspended session. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 7 lead, item 5.5.
FACT: shipped MCP `lenny/send_message` makes no primary call between the target `deps.Store.Get` (read pool) and `deps.Executor.Send`; REST already reads the tenant from the primary before delivery, so the fallback only changes failover behavior on MCP. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:268,479; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:352-357 `Get` on `s.read`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages.go:211-213.
CORRECTS [non-spec.3.fix-G1.1 WATCHOUT "Do not add a Postgres-outage delivery assertion at tier 8"] and [non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1 DECISION "no tier-8 Postgres-outage delivery test"]: their "fail-open is unreachable" premise is gone once the classification fallback lands; the remaining reason not to add tier 8 is the e2e Postgres being emptyDir. Standing context **Re-route read.** stays true (the re-read after `ErrSessionSuspended` still uses `ReadPrimary` with no fallback).
OPEN: test-coverage.md maps failover to tier 8. With the fail-open now reachable on MCP, a later test-coverage round may file a tier-8 in-process case (Postgres container behind `blackholeProxy`, `newStreamChaosGateway`) asserting an MCP message to a `running` session is delivered while the primary is partitioned. Not staged here.
WATCHOUT: the fallback inherits the accepted window already recorded in **Admission fail-open window.**: a lagging read-pool `running` row on a held-pod idle-suspended session is delivered unrecorded during a failover. Do not add a marker for it.

### [non-spec.5.review-performance.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: the message paths' `ReadPrimary` classification read (Design rule 1, CODE-6 item 7) and `DeliverHeld`'s `ReadPrimary` put a primary-Postgres round trip ahead of every path-2 `executor.Send`. During the §12.3 failover window, every MCP `lenny/send_message` to a running session therefore fails. Today it delivers, because the MCP path reads `deps.Store.Get` (read pool) and writes nothing to the primary before `Send`. This also makes staged SPEC-5f's admission fail-open ("existing sessions continue during a Postgres failover") unreachable. Remedy is non-spec: on a non-`ErrNotFound` `ReadPrimary` error, classify the read-pool row, and treat a `DeliverHeld` row-read failure as a held-read failure (return nil). BECAUSE it closes the OPENs at [non-spec.3.fix-G1.1] and [non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1], which no round filed. ALTERNATIVES: filing the REST path as a regression (rejected: `requireTenantNotSuspended` already reads `tenants.Get` on the primary pool, `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:276` `tenantpg.New(pool)`, so REST already fails during a failover today); narrowing the SPEC-5f sentence (rejected: that is a spec edit and leaves the MCP regression in place).
FACT: the shipped REST message path already depends on the Postgres primary through `requireTenantNotSuspended` → `tenantpg.Store.Get` (`s.pool`, no cache). The shipped MCP `send_message` path to a running session makes no primary call before `Executor.Send`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/quota.go:150; pkg/gateway/environment/tenantstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:267-269; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:268,479
USEFUL [non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1 OPEN]: it named the failover conflict precisely, which saved deriving it.
WATCHOUT: before claiming a REST-path failover regression, check `requireTenantNotSuspended`, because a primary read there predates 0092. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages.go:225

FACT: go-redis v9 retries a read timeout on a non-blocking command (retryTimeout set when cmd.readTimeout()==nil), so under the tier-8 blackhole partition each Peek/PeekOldest fails after about 4 x 3 s with default options; redisconn sets no ReadTimeout or MaxRetries, and the fixture's deliver() allows 60 s, so TestHeldReadOutageDoesNotStallDelivery_spec_7_2 passes with defaults — EVIDENCE: pkg/redisconn/redisconn.go:204-223 (no timeout fields), tests/tier8_chaos/stream_failure_chaos_test.go:122-130 (60 s request timeout)
FACT: the Sweeper's evictBinding only drops the registry binding and releases no pod or credential, so a takeover never triggers a stale ReleaseThrough on the old replica — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:248-253
FACT: Binder.Resume always claims an idle pod through connect, so a checkpoint restore after a suspension release cannot land on the released session's still-claimed pod; the cross-replica same-pod ReleaseSlot teardown (UNVERIFIED in Open) needs a snapshotless rebuild, which a completed suspension release (checkpoint at step 2) never leads to — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go:128-145
USEFUL [Standing context, Traps **Commit repeat pacing.**]: saved re-filing the unpaced commit repeat.

### [non-spec.5.review-security.1]
FACT: The shipped renewal path has no session-liveness check: `credRenewalWiring.Renew` (cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:162) and `Worker.Tick` (pkg/gateway/credentials/credrenewal/credrenewal.go:257) never consult session state, and `Worker.Forget` has no production caller. The spec's §4.9 renewal step 1 "Check session liveness" (spec/04_system-components.md:1543) was never implemented. SPEC-1g3's lease-held check (CODE-4 item 23) is the first stop condition the tree gets, so do not file SPEC-1g3 as a regression of an implemented control. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:162-200
FACT: `EvictBinding` (cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams.go:81) only drops the registry entry and the cached stream, and releases no credential lease. A stale replica's eviction therefore cannot revoke the leases a peer's re-adopted pod is using; only `Release`/`ReleaseSlot` (now `ReleaseThrough`) do. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams.go:81-88
FACT: REST message paths run no PreMessageDelivery interceptor today (the only call site is mcptools_register.go:410), so holding a REST message in the DLQ bypasses no interceptor that ran before. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:410
FACT: The erasure `SessionEraser.DeleteBySession` receives (ctx, tenantID, sessionID) (pkg/gateway/storage/erasure/erasure.go:105), and the messaging coordinator is built on the `RedisConcernSessionData` client (cmd/lenny-gateway/session_messaging.go:95), so CODE-6 item 17's `EraseSession` matches the SPEC-5aa `RedisShard(tenantID, RedisConcernSessionData)` routing.
DECISION: no security findings in round 5 — BECAUSE every security bound the proposal touches (active age, retryCount, SuspendedAt lifetime, lease generation, binding stamp) is gateway-written in Postgres or gateway memory, never pod-reported; off-holder held-pod resume and the lease gate fail closed; credential delivery isolation runs on the checkpoint resume and is tested; the generation-scoped release keeps a peer restore's leases (TestStaleReplicaReleaseKeepsPeerRestoreLeases, tier-7a race, tier-9 fresh-lease test) — ALTERNATIVES: considered filing the renewal-race replacement lease that outlives a podless terminal session, rejected because the proposal already records it as an accepted edge (non-spec Edge cases, **Renewal racing a release by identifier.**) and the OPEN entry already carries it.

CODE-6 item 2.2 and item 16.3 cite it as `holdsPod`. CODE-6 item 7's REST bullet instead restates it as "only when the locked row is `suspended` and names no pod". That wording came in with redesign5 commit 022f4b9e5 and differs from the home on a gateway with no pod binder. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:125, :194, :215, :237

A non-spec site that describes a guard should cite the item that implements it. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:120 (`holdsPod` definition)

### [non-spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: add one case, `TestPrimaryFailoverDeliversToRunningSession_spec_7_2`, to the TEST-1 Tier 8 bullet, injecting a failover (primary proxy closes its connections and refuses new dials) rather than a `blackholeProxy` partition — BECAUSE CODE-6 item 7 claims delivery "through a §12.3 failover", which is a fast-erroring primary; under a partition the classification `ReadPrimary` and `StampSync` hang until the request context ends (Standing context **Admission fail-open window.**: `StampSync` has no timeout), so the reviewer's partition test would fail against the staged design — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's `blackholeProxy` partition (encodes behavior the design does not provide, or forces a new operator-tunable primary-read timeout mechanism); closing the primary `pgxpool` in-process (errors fast but is not a network failover); adding an MCP surface to `newStreamChaosGateway` (larger fixture; REST and MCP share the classification fallback and the `StampSync` fail-open).
FACT: `newStreamChaosGateway` hard-codes `memstore.New()` and wires no tenant store, so a REST request reaches classification with the primary down; in production `requireTenantNotSuspended` reads `s.tenants` first and answers 500 on its error. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier8_chaos/stream_failure_chaos_test.go:89,100; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/quota.go:146-158
FACT: `blackholeProxy` keeps both connections open and drops bytes, so a pgx query through it blocks until its context ends rather than erroring. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier8_chaos/stream_failure_chaos_test.go:387-440
OPEN: under a primary partition (hang, not failover) the message path blocks on `ReadPrimary`/`StampSync` while holding the delivery lock until the request context ends. Whether that needs a bound is a design question for a later round or the owner; the staged design makes no claim about it.
CORRECTS [non-spec.5.fix-design-G2.1 OPEN]: closed by the tier-8 failover case above; the partition variant stays open as stated.

### [non-spec.6.review-performance.1]
DECISION: returned no findings in non-spec round 6. BECAUSE the r5-to-r6 delta has three parts: the CODE-6 item 7 read-pool fallback on a non-`ErrNotFound` `ReadPrimary` error, the `DeliverHeld` read-error-returns-nil clause, and two test additions (the `BindingGeneration` production test and the failing-`Update` case of `TestMessagePathsClassifyPrimaryRow`). None of them adds a store write, a watch, or a store dependency. Under a §12.3 failover the MCP path-2 trace is: fallback row `running`, then a `DeliverHeld` read failure (WARN, nil), then the `StampSync` fail-open, so it delivers as shipped. A stale held row on the fallback goes through `holdMessage`, whose failed re-read answers `inbox_unavailable`, which fails closed and loses nothing. ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing per-message primary cost (two `ReadPrimary` row-lock transactions plus the uncoalesced `StampSync` `UPDATE` per MCP path-2 send, against the §12.3 Tier 3 budget of ~300/s session-state writes and ~1,300/s total). Rejected: OD-A (owner) settled the per-Send write and redesign 6 settled the primary read. (2) Filing hang-versus-fail latency of the primary calls under a blackholed primary (pgconn's default dialer has no connect timeout). Rejected: `StampSync` has the same exposure and it was accepted under OD-A. (3) Filing the delegate_task `DeliverHeld` read failure that leaves a running child's held entries until the next delivery point. Rejected: this is the same class as the accepted **Held read failure.** outcome.
FACT: CODE-4 item 23.2's `LeaseStore.Lookup` returns a scan, KMS, or decode error as an error rather than as not-found, so a Postgres failover makes renewal retry rather than drop leases through `ErrLeaseReleased`. The Traps line "scanLease folds errors into not-found" describes the old scan path. It does not describe `Lookup`. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 23.2
FACT: shipped `Stamper.Stamp` coalesces to at most one async write per second per session with a 5 s persist timeout, while `StampSync` "never skips the write for coalescing". The per-Send primary write rate is therefore new with OD-A. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionidle/sessionidle.go:139,174-184,228; non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 5.1
USEFUL [non-spec.5.review-performance.1]: its REST `requireTenantNotSuspended` WATCHOUT saved re-deriving the REST failover baseline.

### [spec-recheck.1.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings — BECAUSE the spec delta since spec-r2-prefix (SPEC-1a step 3e minimal-state carve-out, SPEC-1c Retry policy reduced to a §7.3 pointer, SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** "A replica that acquires the Lease evaluates the condition at once") introduces no recovery path that loses, double-applies, leaks, stampedes, or stalls; the failover bound now has its mechanism (evaluate on acquisition), and a pass overlapping a deposed leader is harmless because the `suspended → resume_pending` write is guarded on `suspended` plus a podless row — ALTERNATIVES: filing the leader re-check's use of a listed row's `SuspendedAt` (read authority is the non-spec Design rule's job, and a stale list only yields a guarded no-op or an early resume).

FACT: credential leases traced across suspend, release, and resume hold under SPEC-1g's generation scope: a podless resume passes the adopter's takeover CAS and the driver's entry increment, so a restore's leases always record more than any earlier binding's stamp, including when a stale releaser's suspension release (clear, migrate, release) overlaps the peer's restore. A late migration from that releaser lands after the new coordinator's delivery point, and SPEC-5i "A DLQ entry held after the coordinating replica last delivered held messages is delivered at the next of those points" plus idle suspension and backlog resume delivers it.
FACT: the driver's claim-retry pacing is a non-spec carrier (CODE-4 item 12, jittered `--resume-driver-backoff-seconds`), so SPEC-1b item 1's "attempts again later" is not an unpaced spec retry — EVIDENCE: review-log-archive.md:421

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: on a `ReadPrimary` error other than `ErrNotFound`, `DeliverHeld` falls back to the `s.store.Get` row and returns that read's error — BECAUSE staged §7.2 **Delivery of held messages.** exempts only inbox/DLQ read failures, and the MCP path treats a `DeliverHeld` error as an error of the new message's Send, so both reads failing fails the path-2 message instead of letting it overtake held entries — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "call deliverBuffered anyway" (no row to pass; deliverBuffered needs row.ID/TenantID; its "Send fails in streamFor or the admitter" justification is false under SPEC-5f admitter fail-open); return nil after fallback fails (ordering violation); no fallback (breaks path 2 through a §12.3 failover); change DeliverHeld to take the caller's row (delegate_task has no row).
CORRECTS [review-log DECISION "`DeliverHeld` treats its own `ReadPrimary` error as a held read failure (WARN, nil)"]: a session-row read failure is not an inbox/DLQ read failure; returning nil let a new path-2 message overtake readable DLQ entries.
WATCHOUT: the re-read after `sessionidle.ErrSessionSuspended` stays fallback-free; do not extend the read-pool fallback to it — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 7 lead.
WATCHOUT: `TestMessagePathsClassifyPrimaryRow_spec_7_2`'s failing-`Update` case must seed a DLQ entry; without one it passes on the ordering violation.

CORRECTS [review-log.md:337 FACT on bufferAfterResumedSendFailure, archive:4987]: "Do not file it as an unlisted site unless the phrase is narrowed" — the site is now named explicitly; the ledger guidance is moot.
FACT: the existing test for the resumed-send-failure fresh-state path is `TestResumeAndDeliverSendFailureAfterReportUsesFreshState_spec_7_2` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_resume_deliver_test.go:326; the only caller of `bufferAfterResumedSendFailure` is messages_delivery.go:202, after `executor.Send` fails on the resumed row.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: `DeliverHeld` on a `ReadPrimary` error other than `ErrNotFound` falls back to the `s.store.Get` (read-pool) row and delivers when that row is `running`; an error of that fallback read is returned, so the MCP path handles it as an error of the new message's `executor.Send` (§7.2 **Delivery of held messages.**: the path-2 message is handled as if its own delivery failed). BECAUSE `deliverBuffered(ctx, row)` needs a real row (`recordDeliveredResponse` uses `row.ID`, `row.TenantID`, and `recordTTFTOnce(row, ...)`), and this mirrors the CODE-6 item 7 classification fallback that already lets a `running` target keep path-2 delivery through a §12.3 failover. ALTERNATIVES: (a) the reviewer's literal fix, call `deliverBuffered` on the error (rejected: no row to pass, and its justification "Send to a non-running row fails in streamFor or the admitter" is false during a failover, where the item 5.5 admitter fails open per SPEC-5f); (b) change `DeliverHeld` to take the caller's classified row (rejected: `delegate_task` has only the `session.State` that `MaterializeDelegatedChild` returns, and it cascades into rule 1 and `TestDeliverHeldReadsPrimary_spec_7_2`); (c) return the `ReadPrimary` error at once (rejected: fails every MCP path-2 message through a primary failover, undoing the settled classification fallback); (d) return nil on the fallback read's failure too (rejected: the new message then overtakes unread-but-held entries, which the spec's exemption covers only for an inbox or DLQ read failure).
FACT: `recordDeliveredResponse` reads row fields beyond IDs (`recordTTFTOnce(row, "response")`). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:309-368.
CORRECTS [review-log DECISION at review-log.md:420 "`DeliverHeld` treats its own `ReadPrimary` error as a held read failure (WARN, nil)"]: a row read failure is not an inbox or DLQ read failure under §7.2 **Delivery of held messages.**; returning nil lets the MCP path-2 message overtake readable DLQ entries. The fallback-then-return-error rule above replaces it.
WATCHOUT: the rule-1 parenthesis in Design **Session-row read authority.** ("the first of which falls back to the read-pool row") counts which reads fall back; it must change with any fallback added or removed. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:17.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.review-performance.1]
DECISION: returned no findings in non-spec-recheck round 1 — BECAUSE the delta since spec-r2-prefix (CODE-6 item 7 read-pool fallback, `DeliverHeld` read-error nil, `handleInterrupt` mutator sentinel, CODE-1/CODE-6 comment and openapi targets, `Run` calls `Pass` at start, tier-8 failover and Redis-partition tests, summary reductions and OD-S1) adds no store write, watch, or per-unit cost beyond what non-spec.6 already weighed, and every failover trace (Postgres failover, Redis reset, coordinator handoff, leader handoff) degrades no worse than shipped — ALTERNATIVES: (1) per-message primary cost: `ReadPrimary` is `Update` with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` then rollback, which takes a row lock (WAL heap-lock record, xid) per call; REST path 2 pays 1 plus `StampSync`, MCP pays 2 plus `StampSync`. Spec gives no message rate, only gateway→pod RPC ~2,000/s at Tier 3 (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:263) against a ~1,600 IOPS sustained primary ceiling (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:115-121). Not filed: `StampSync` per Send is owner-settled (summary Decision 16, OD-A) and is the same order, and non-spec.6 rejected the read cost as settled by redesign 6; a plain primary-pool SELECT would cut the lock cost without changing the rule if a later owner review wants it. (2) Redis outage holding idle-suspended pods (`HeldLen` error returns true, no release): no worse than shipped, which held the idle pod to the 7200 s idle bound.
FACT: the watchdog already issues one `List` per state per tenant per tick (5 s, `DefaultTickInterval`) and `sweepMaxAge` lists every row, so `sweepSuspended`'s listing adds one indexed state query per tenant per tick and podless rows were already listed every tick — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:166, :588-604, :1074-1075
FACT: §16 documents a Tier 4 (100,000 sessions, 2,000/s creation, 5,000 tenants) above Tier 3; the leader re-check at Tier 4 is ~100,000 `HeldLen` calls plus 5,000 tenant listings per 300 s pass (~350 Redis ops/s), within one pass interval at ~1 ms per call — EVIDENCE: spec/16_observability.md:595-598
USEFUL [non-spec.6.review-performance.1]: its rejected-alternatives list (per-message primary cost, blackholed-primary hang) saved re-deriving both and placed them under OD-A and redesign 6.
USEFUL [Settled: Leader capacity.]: bound held at Tier 3; extended above to Tier 4.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: the REST path-2 `deliverBuffered` call reads no session row (it peeks the inbox and DLQ only), while the MCP path-2 call goes through `SessionDelivery.DeliverHeld`, which does its own `ReadPrimary` first. Under a Postgres primary error the two paths therefore diverge. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 6 (Loop bullet), CODE-6 item 7 `SessionDelivery` bullet.
WATCHOUT: the new "a `ReadPrimary` error is logged at WARN and returns nil" in `DeliverHeld` makes the MCP path-2 message overtake readable held DLQ entries during a primary failover. SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** exempts only an inbox or DLQ read failure from the held-before-path-2 order. Filed as a finding; the remedy is to call `deliverBuffered` on a read error rather than return nil (a non-running row then fails in `streamFor` or the admitter and leaves the entry at the head). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:217, spec-changes.md:531.
FACT: `gatewayleader.Gate.Run` re-invokes every job's `Run` on each `OnStartedLeading`, so CODE-6 item 16.3's "`Run` calls `Pass` once when it starts" does give evaluation at once on every Lease acquisition. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go:222-229, :142-147.
FACT: `Binder.Release` releases credentials before the teardown (release.go:66), so a step-4 teardown error after `releasePodCredentials` does not leave the pre-suspension leases renewing.

DECISION: returned no findings in non-spec-recheck round 3 — BECAUSE the r1-prefix delta is three edits (Design rule 1 adds `bufferAfterResumedSendFailure` as a `ReadPrimary` site and names `sweepIdle` through `SuspendIdle`; `DeliverHeld` falls back to the `s.store.Get` row on a non-`ErrNotFound` `ReadPrimary` error and returns that read's error; two test additions). The only new store cost is one read-pool `Get` on the `DeliverHeld` primary-error path, which runs only during a primary outage. FACT: shipped `bufferAfterResumedSendFailure` reads `s.store.Get` (read pool) and buffers to the inbox on a re-read error. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:246-261

### [non-spec-recheck.3.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in non-spec-recheck round 3. BECAUSE the diff against non-spec-recheck-r1-prefix is the same four hunks that round 2 reviewed: rule 1 names the `bufferAfterResumedSendFailure` re-read, rule 2 adds `sweepIdle` through `SuspendIdle`, `DeliverHeld` falls back to `s.store.Get`, and the failing-`Update` and fresh-state tests were extended. No staging changed after round 2. A whole-document walk of CODE-4 items 1-24, CODE-5, CODE-6 items 2, 4, 5, 7, and 16, and the Design paragraphs found no non-idempotent replay, unreclaimed resource, or unbounded wait that earlier rounds had not already settled. ALTERNATIVES: filing the adopter `TurnInFlight` gap (see the OPEN below). I rejected it because it is narrow and it improves on shipped behaviour, which expires the session terminally on any replica. Its remedy is also an owner trade-off between holding the pods of adopted idle sessions and suspending a silent turn, and neither choice is a required fix.
CORRECTS [Standing context Open, "UNVERIFIED: whether an adopter's `TurnInFlight` can idle-suspend a turn the dead coordinator started"]: verified. It can, but only for a turn that produces no other qualifying activity. On the sidecar transport, `Attach` returns on `ctx.Done` and does not signal the runtime, so the turn keeps running after the coordinator dies. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/attach.go:186-187, and the heartbeat comment at attach.go:70-73. In the tree, the only stamps outside the proxy, direct-mode, and await_children paths are the `response`/`response_degraded` publishes. They happen after `Send` returns on the replica that sent the turn. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/events.go:362-369, messages_delivery.go:361,376. Proxy-mode chunks are stamped on whichever replica serves the proxy. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/llmproxy_usage.go:250-253. A turn that keeps calling the LLM is therefore protected after a handoff. A turn running a long tool with no LLM traffic for `maxClientIdleSeconds` after its admission stamp is not protected: the adopter's `SuspendIdle` sees no cached conn, suspends the session, and the zero hold releases the pod mid-turn.
OPEN (owner): summary.md:47 says the lease gate makes `SuspendIdle` correct. That stops holding after a handoff, because the new lease holder does not hold the turn. One possible remedy is a `TurnInFlight` that reports "unknown" (true) when this replica has no cached conn for an adopted `running` session. Its cost is that every idle session adopted after a rolling update keeps its pod until its next message or until the active-age cap. That trade-off is the owner's to make.
USEFUL [non-spec-recheck.2.review-reliability.1]: its trace of `recordDeliveredResponse` writing no session row settled the failing-`Update` test case without re-deriving it.

### [spec.5.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5f close rule restated as "one `response` for each message it wrote on that stream", with one sentence accepting the window where a pre-stream turn's `response` closes a later message's turn early — BECAUSE the stdout `response` frame carries no message id (spec/28 `response` fields; schemas/lenny-adapter.proto AttachResponse carries only envelope_json) and CODE-6 item 5.2 already closes on conn quiescence — ALTERNATIVES: a correlation field on `response` (wire change for every runtime); a skip-first-close on a conn opened over a foreign open turn (new state, pins adopted sessions).
DECISION: fail-open admission (OD-A) keeps delivering; SPEC-5f now states the delivering replica does not suspend while that delivery is in progress, and that other replicas and an earlier committed suspend write do not see it, so the release can end that turn — BECAUSE CODE-6 item 5.3 already applies the TurnInFlight check, and the adopter claim was false for unrecorded admissions — ALTERNATIVES: refuse on store error (contradicts §12.3 continuity); a persisted marker (row unwritable during failover).
WATCHOUT: the idle predicate has one home, SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**; the SPEC-5a and SPEC-5h state-block lines and summary Decision 10 carry no copy of it. Do not reinsert "with no turn in flight" there. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5a/SPEC-5h code lines.
WATCHOUT: the vocabulary for the stays-open case is "a turn that no replica marks closed" (covers lost coordinator, ended stream, failed close write). The non-spec Edge label **Turn end no replica received.** was deliberately not renamed; its body already names the `CloseTurn` failure.
OPEN: non-spec-changes.md TEST tier-8 `TestCoordinatorKillMidTurnNotIdleSuspended_spec_6_2` diagnosis says "a turn a dead coordinator started"; it remains true for that fixture (admission recorded before the kill) but the non-spec loop may align it to "whose admission the dead coordinator recorded".

### [spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the turn-close rule is restated as a count ("its stream has received one `response` for each message it wrote"), and the window where a `response` from a turn started before that stream opened is counted for a later message is accepted in one SPEC-5f sentence — BECAUSE `response` carries no message id (spec/28_communication-channels.md `response` fields; schemas/lenny-adapter.proto AttachRequest/AttachResponse), the staged executor already closes on quiescence (CODE-6 item 5.2), and the early close needs a turn silent for maxClientIdleSeconds after a coordinator loss or stream end — ALTERNATIVES: message correlation on CH-ATTACH (wire change for every runtime); fail-safe "first response on a stream opened over an open turn closes nothing" (needs a prior-open return from StampSync plus conn state, and pins an adopted session that gets one more message until the next message or maxSessionAge, the pinning the review log already rejected).
FACT: on one conn the executor serializes turns with a one-slot token, so a second Send waits for the first `response`; finding 1's case (c) (path-4 overlap on one stream) does not occur in the staged code, and the count rule covers it anyway — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/podstream.go acquireTurn/completeTurn (token cap 1), pod.go Send doc comment.
DECISION: the fail-open delivery keeps OD-A's fail-open; SPEC-5f now states the delivering replica's no-suspend-while-delivering condition (the code's `TurnInFlight` check, CODE-6 item 5.3) and the accepted outcome for an adopter or an earlier committed suspension — BECAUSE the condition is a pod-release predicate the code already applies, so it belongs in the spec — ALTERNATIVES: refusing delivery on admission failure (contradicts §12.3 failover continuity and the settled **Admission fail-open.**).
DECISION: state-block labels drop "with no turn in flight" rather than switch to "no turn open" — BECAUSE SPEC-5f is the one home of the predicate and both labels already sit beside or point at §6.2.

### [spec.5.review-docs-alignment.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: SPEC-5f states the admission fail-open but not its outcome, namely that an unrecorded delivery opens no turn on the row, so a `running → suspended` write that landed first (stale read-pool `running` row during a §12.3 failover) or one made by an adopter with no stream is not stopped, and the zero-hold release can end that turn after a `delivered` receipt. FACT: the suspension release (non-spec CODE-6 item 4 steps 1-5) checks no `TurnInFlight` and no `TurnOpen`; only `SuspendIdle` does (CODE-6 item 5.3). Any turn that the row does not record is unprotected at release time. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 items 4 and 5.3.
FACT: the "hang mid-turn" Edge-cases row holds: a runtime that misses `heartbeat_ack` makes the adapter end the stream with `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED`, which §28.8 `CH-ATTACH` classes as a stream failure, so the restore commit closes the turn. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:578, :1941.

### [spec.5.review-fresh.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: SPEC-5f's turn-close rule ("the runtime's `response` to the last message it wrote") cannot be evaluated after a delivery path 4 write on the SAME stream, because path 4 writes a second message while a turn is in flight and `response` carries no message id — BECAUSE the earlier turn's `response` then closes the row boundary of the path-4 turn, and idle suspension can release the pod while that turn runs silent past `maxClientIdleSeconds` — ALTERNATIVES: folding it into [spec.5.review-mechanism.1]'s fresh-stream finding (rejected: that finding's evidence covers only fresh streams, so a fix scoped to adopters and post-stream-end replicas leaves path 4 open).
FACT: the shipped executor serializes turns per stream with a token (`attachConn.registerTurn`/`completeTurn`, pkg/gateway/session/executor/podstream.go:151-192), so code cannot express path 4's mid-turn write today; the spec still states it (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:331, spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1602). EVIDENCE: as cited
FACT: the stdout `response` frame schema has `type`, `output`/`text`, `sessionId`, optional `error`, and no message id; the stdin `message` has `id`. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:613-623, 753-766
WATCHOUT: any remedy for turn-close correlation must fail safe (keep the turn open on ambiguity). Closing early is the direction that releases a pod mid-turn; staying open only costs a pod until `maxSessionAge`.
UNVERIFIED: an interrupt suspension leaves `turn_open` true, and §15.4.3 Basic/Standard have "no clean interrupt" (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1778), and INTERRUPT_TIMEOUT "assumes" the runtime stopped (spec/28_communication-channels.md:1208). So whether a held-pod resume after an interrupt can ever be idle-suspended depends on runtime behaviour the spec does not state. Two other lenses in this round recorded the same thing. Not filed.
USEFUL [spec.5.review-mechanism.1]: its CH-ATTACH correlation FACT (no correlating frame, reader completes the registered turn on the first `response`) let me skip re-deriving the frame schema.
