### [spec.13.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in spec round 13 — BECAUSE crash, store-error, and takeover paths through the redesign-10 idle suspension (SPEC-5f, SPEC-5ab) all end in a bounded state. (1) A crash between seal and write leaves the seal. The peer adopts the `running` row (SPEC-2a). Refused messages do not reset `last_agent_activity_at` (SPEC-5e), so the adopter's next idle firing re-seals at count 0 and completes. (2) A crash after the write leaves the pod unreferenced, and orphan-claim GC reclaims it. The leader re-check covers the backlog, and in-memory inbox loss is already accepted. (3) A stale replica's late `UnsealSession` after a takeover cannot lift the adopter's seal, because the pod rejects any gateway-to-pod RPC with a stale generation. — ALTERNATIVES: filing a stale replica's checkpoint-failure unseal racing the adopter's suspension (rejected: generation fencing on every pod RPC); filing the interrupt hold release (SPEC-5b), which has no generation conjunct (rejected: the row is `suspended`, so no delivery is made, and SPEC-1f item 3 covers a release by a replica that lost the lease); filing messages held behind a seal that was not lifted (rejected: latency bounded by the next idle firing or `maxSessionAge`, already refuted).
FACT: the pod validates `coordination_generation` on every gateway-to-pod RPC and rejects a stale one, so `SealSession`/`UnsealSession` from a replica that lost the session are refused once the adopter's `CoordinatorFence` lands — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:237-238
FACT: concurrent idle attempts on one replica at one generation are excluded by the per-replica in-flight record that `SuspendIdle` shares with `ReleaseSuspendedPod` — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 **`SuspendIdle`.** ("when a release or an idle suspension of the session is in flight on this replica")
USEFUL [Standing **Refuted: store-outage degradation.**]: it saved refiling the loss of `suspended` DLQ entries held in Redis for 7 days.

### [spec.13.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding on the staged spec edits — BECAUSE every changed control checked holds: the suspension release drains a non-recycling pod and recycles a tenant-pinned one (stricter than the current "back to the pool", spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:229); §12.8 erasure gains the DLQ and durable-inbox keys before SessionStore through the existing `RedisConcernSessionData` concern (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:411); BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED stays sentinel-detected and fail-closed (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:408); generation-scoped lease release only ever revokes extra (never leaks a lease that a restore recorded) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the adapter turn count as a pod self-report bound (rejected: it guards the session's own pod release, not a cross-session or residual-state bound, and maxSessionAge still caps a runtime that withholds `response`).
FACT: erasure verification in §12.8 checks only the erasure salt, so the SPEC-5aa scope row needs no verification-step edit — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:895
WATCHOUT: SPEC-1g's "never returns a lease that a later restore assigned" holds only for restore-recorded and replacement leases; a zero-recorded lease assigned mid-session after a restore can be revoked by a stale release. That direction over-revokes (availability), not a leak; do not file it as a security regression.

### [spec.13.review-single-source.1]
FACT: SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** states the SPEC-5ab seal contract twice in its own paragraph: "The adapter seals the session only when no turn is outstanding, and while the session is sealed it writes no message to the runtime, whichever replica delivers it" (right after the §4.7.1 **Session seal.** citation), and again in the safety argument, "The adapter places the seal only when no turn is outstanding". SPEC-5ab item 2 is the home ("The adapter refuses it while a turn is outstanding ... While a session is sealed, the adapter writes no `message` frame"). Filed under (g); the fix deletes both SPEC-5f copies and keeps the citations. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f and SPEC-5ab item 2
DECISION: did not file "Another suspension attempt lifts the seal only after a takeover" (SPEC-5f) against SPEC-5ab's "the next idle firing on its coordinating replica seals it again" — BECAUSE it holds if "attempt" is read as scoped to one coordinator generation, which is how the caller frames it; a same-replica repeat after a failed UnsealSession is serialized by the in-flight record and does not break safety — ALTERNATIVES: filing it as a (c) contradiction (ambiguous, likely refuted).
DECISION: did not file the DLQ scoring rule at three sites (dead-letter table, migration step 2, §12.4 key table), the §28.5.1 Timing restatement of the 5 s deadline, or the Design-section route list — BECAUSE they follow existing spec conventions (key registry, channel cards) or are a summary description of a deliverable.
WATCHOUT: the Edge-cases bullet "No outcome releases a pod with a turn outstanding, including during a Postgres failover" repeats the SPEC-5f sentence "The release never ends a turn, including during a Postgres failover"; it is one clause and was not filed. If SPEC-5f's invariant wording changes, change that bullet too or reduce it to a pointer. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md Edge cases, "An adapter that refuses the seal" bullet
